7 Examining the Factors Influencing AI Adoption in the Insurance Industry Specifically in Actuarial Process

Arwin Sandesh D’Souza, Daksha Malagatti, Ravi Prakash Bylappa, and Rama Moorthy Hejamdi

DOI: 10.1201/9781003472551-9

7.1 Meaning of Actuarial Science

Actuarial science is the study of the certainty or uncertainty of a natural event by using statistical and mathematical models as well as the assessment of the degree of risks in order to control the intensity of monetary losses to either client or the company by formulating different policies.

Actuarial science is mainly used to calculate the future financial impact of an uncertain event. Actuarial science is used to create models for real-world problems and to consider the events that are happening, can happen, or might happen, and it tries to analyze the risk associated with the aforesaid event. Actuarial science is useful in measuring, managing, and understanding the risks at present as well as the risks in future.

Complex problems related to risk management are solved, assessed, and predicted, and the monetary impact of these problems is evaluated using the concept of actuarial science.

Insurance companies predict the probability of the uncertain event and estimate the amount that needs to be provided as claims to their customers by using tools and techniques of actuarial science.

Usually, actuarial science is associated with insurance and finance, but it is not limited to one single field. The application of actuarial science is endless since most events in the world are part of a probabilistic and deterministic system.

Actuarial science derives its main concepts from the fields of statistics, economics, mathematics, finance, accounts, and computer science. Actuarial science also benefits from any advancements in the aforementioned fields as well.

Actuarial science rose to prominence in the 17th century. When it was introduced, it was a part of mathematics discipline. The 17th century saw a rise in the insurance sector, hence the process of calculation of risks associated with providing insurance also found its own significance.

Actuarial science has developed and grown in importance in the past few decades due to the improvements in technology of data storage and analysis, dynamic development of financial theories and models, as well as steady and massive increments of development in information technology.

Different kinds of risks are evaluated in actuarial science:

  • Financial risks: Risks associated with finance.
    • Credit risk: Risk of defaulting of one of the parties to a contract in discharging or fulfilling financial obligations.
    • Market risk: Risk of losing money in an investment because of various events which take place in the external business environment. Most of the factors which are impactful in market risks are beyond the control of individuals and corporations. For example, economic policies, changes in interest rates, fluctuation in exchange rates, and uncertain events like terrorist attacks or a global pandemic.
  • Insurance risks: Risks associated with insurance.
    • Trade risk: Risk of defaulting by the proposer of the insurance on premium payments.
    • Technical life risk: Risk of death or survival of the proposer of the insurance.
    • Morbidity risk: Risk of disability and health of the proposer of the insurance.
    • Non-life risk: Automobile, home, and casualty risks.

Far more important is the mean risk. This refers to the sum of all possible operating losses, each multiplied by their probabilities. This definition is well defined, permits no uncertainty, and can be used to compute the mean risk for a given insurance portfolio (Sherris 2006).

7.2 Artificial Intelligence in Actuarial Science

Artificial intelligence (AI) has revolutionized numerous industries, which include actuarial technological know-how, with the aid of enhancing methods and enabling extra accurate danger evaluation strategies. Actuarial technology is often used to study economic risks and uncertainties using mathematical and statistical techniques. Machine learning, a subset of AI, has observed substantial software in this domain, imparting sophisticated algorithms that expect destiny occasions, determine risks, and optimize selection-making tactics.

One of the fundamental packages of AI in actuarial technological know-how is in risk assessment. Traditional actuarial methods rely heavily on ancient statistics and statistical fashions to estimate future dangers. However, device mastering strategies increase those strategies by studying enormous datasets more effectively and identifying complicated styles that won’t be apparent via conventional analysis.

Machine gaining knowledge of algorithms, which include neural networks, selection bushes, and random forests, can process massive volumes of structured and unstructured statistics to identify correlations and trends. In risk evaluation, these algorithms can forecast historical claims facts, demographic statistical data, marketplace trends, and different relevant elements to expect the chance of future events, which include insurance claims, mortality charges, or funding returns. As an example, in insurance, system mastering fashions can anticipate policyholder statistics to examine the hazard of future claims primarily based on diverse hazard factors which include age, level of fitness, occupation, and lifestyle patterns. By using these predictions, insurers are able to alter their rates, ensure that they are competitive in the market, and sufficiently address resource issues.

In addition, tools acquire strategic knowledge to enhance risk categorization. Subgroups within a population are determined with identical risk profiles. This categorization allows insurers to customize their products and pricing techniques to better meet client’s satisfaction. Furthermore, it responds to the demands of different categories of customers, ultimately increasing the profitability and pride of the clients. Additionally, enhanced AI-powered predictive analytics can benefit actuaries become more informed. Emerging risks like cybersecurity threats and weather-related business incidents are evaluated. Machine learning can be sued by analyzing enormous datasets and integrating real-time statistics. This approach can provide early risks about capacity risks and provide it to insurance firms and financial institutions, an organization that quickly counters and manages these types of hazards.

Another important use of AI in actuarial technology is fraud detection. Insurance fraud damages a massive economic burden on the enterprise, costing billions of greenbacks yearly. Algorithms for device monitoring can scan claim data for patterns that reveal anomalies or questionable activity, which could be signs of fraud. Those algorithms can flag possibly fraudulent claims for further investigation, assisting insurers reduce losses and hold the integrity of their operations. Moreover, AI can improve the performance of actuarial methods by automating repetitive tasks and reducing the reliance on manual calculations. As an example, machine learning algorithms can streamline underwriting strategies by evaluating applicant information and providing guidelines for hazard evaluation and pricing decisions. In addition, AI-powered tools can streamline actuaries in portfolio optimization, manage asset-liability and capital allocation, and allow extra informed and timely preference-making.

Despite the numerous advantages that AI offers to actuarial technology, many challenges need to be solved. One of the major challenges is this Interpretability of Machine Learning Capabilities. Although these models may provide precise data, the fundamental elements of using those predictions remain. Actuaries can make educational decisions and comply with regulatory requirements. Additionally, records satisfactory and privateness worries are important issues in leveraging AI for hazard evaluation. Actuaries ought to make sure the accuracy, reliability, and privacy of the information used to teach device getting to know fashions to avoid biased outcomes or unauthorized get right of entry to touchy statistics (Embrechts and Wüthrich 2022).

Basic principles and methods are very important for managing uncertainty and making informed decisions in these areas. This facility plays an important role in ensuring the financial stability of insurance companies, pension funds, and other organizations facing risk.

At the core of scientific analysis is the analysis of uncertain future events and their financial consequences.

The principle of risk sharing is a basic tenet of actuarial analysis. Insurance works by spreading risk over many insured individuals. Premiums are decided by actuaries, who do so based on the insured person’s needs, such as age, gender, health status, and past risk experience. By risk pooling, insurance companies are both able to enhance their financial stability and enlarge their revenues, while also offering financial security for the policyholders in the case of unexpected events.

In addition to assessing risks, workers also help in managing debts and assets. ALM is making sure that an organization’s assets match its debts to pay what it owes. In the insurance sectors, this means maintaining the equilibrium between resources and what they need to pay for insurance strategies and benefits to those who are insured. Actuaries use tools like financial plans and risk evaluations to make investment strategies. These strategies evaluate about things like how much money can be made from investments, income, and policy.

Financial liabilities and assets measurement are additional aspects of actuarial science. Estimating the fair value of insurance policies, pension liabilities, and other financial obligations falls under the responsibilities of actuaries. Predicting the upcoming financial circumstances, minimizing them to their current value, and taking into account a variety of risks, including inflation, mortality, and behavior, are all part of this procedure. For insurer and pension companies to comply with regulations and make wise decisions, precise measurement is crucial to their financial reporting.

Actuarial science is a combination of statistics, computer science, economics, and finance. Managing risk is an important task for employees. They assist businesses in identifying, assessing, and reducing the risks associated with the finances, daily activities, and business risks. Actuaries evaluate risk, generate plans to reduce the risk, and make informed choices to benefit the entire firm by employing mathematical models and statistics. Actuaries must have strong analytical skills, critical thinking skills, and a deep understanding of theory to be effective in assessing and managing risk in a complex and uncertain environment (Pathnaik et al. 2024).

7.3 Application and Case Studies from Finance and Insurance

According to a broad understanding of actuarial science, some fields in finance and insurance have been identified, which can immensely benefit from the application of AI to it. They are:

7.3.1 Underwriting

The process of evaluating the risk profile of the proposer of the insurance as well as evaluating the risk profile of an asset is called underwriting. Underwriting is done at multiple levels; the first portion of underwriting and the most primary one is done on-site by the agent. Later on, the underwriting is approved by higher officers in the department.

Most types of life insurance require an estimate of expected lifetime of an individual at the time of application. This is referred to as mortality risk, and the process of collecting and analyzing data that describes such risk is known as underwriting (Maier et al. 2019).

Various information is collected during this process. Some of the information that are collected are listed below:

  • Age group
  • Gender
  • Medical background
  • Family illness history
  • Lifestyle
  • Geographical location

For finance sector, the information collected are

  • Creditworthiness
  • Financial stability
  • Ability to repay
  • Value of collateral asset

After proving the authenticity of the risk analysis, the terms and conditions associated with the contract are decided accordingly. In insurance, the terms generally refer to the premium payments and the duration of these payments as well as the type of payment.

If the underwriting process is accurate, it reduces the financial burden to the insurance companies or lenders. However, if the underwriting process is factually incorrect or flawed, then it can potentially create a lot of financial damage to the company (Figure 7.1).

AI can be useful in providing supplementary information as well as additional indicators to assist in estimation of the risk for the underwriter. AI also requires datasets to be precise, and pre-existing data sets based on previous underwritings can be used to analyze and create a system for providing suggestions, as well as create an early detection and warning system to flag high risk proposals.

AI can also help in providing precise plans based on the exact risk assessment of proposers of insurance. This can result in ultra-customization of plans that are specifically tailored with the variables of individual users.

AI can also be used to provide customer support and collect feedback as customer support in insurance holds a very high importance. An AI-assisted customer support center will be more impactful in dealing with the doubts put forth by the customers and to deal with the feedback of the customers to bring in improvement to the system as well.

7.3.2 Case of Underwriting in India

In India, there are various parameters that need to be followed in order to efficiently proceed with the underwriting process. Different insurance companies have different processes, but the underlying essence still remains the same. The amount of information that needs to be collected from the proposer of the insurance is immense, whether the data is for life insurance, vehicle insurance, or health insurance.

The data that need to be collected by underwriters are then verified by their superior officers as the secondary underwriter. Only after this process can the insurance move ahead. There are various court cases regarding underwriting, which provide valuable insights into where AI can play a role.

7.3.3 Some Examples of These Cases

Case of Mr Hemanthkumar N Vasra v/s Reliance Nippon Life Insurance Co. Ltd. Ref no. AHD-L-036-1718-0086.

In this case, the customer had been insured for a sum of 742,500. The insured had failed to disclose the material facts during the underwriting process. The insured had taken treatment for diabetes as well as ischemic heart complications prior to taking the policy.

The court ruled in the favor of Reliance Nippon life insurance, and the insurance claim was repudiated.

Case of Reliance life insurance limited v/s Rekhaben Nareshbhai Rathod (2019) civil appeal 4261/2009.

In this case, the insured had taken policy by Max New York life insurance for a sum assured of 1,10,000 in the year 2009. During the same year, the insured had enquired with Reliance Life Insurance Limited for a sum insured of 1,00,000. During the underwriting process with Reliance Insurance Limited, the insured had not disclosed the fact about the previously held policy. The insured also agreed to cancel the contract and to forfeit the premium already paid in case the information provided was found to be untrue.

The insured died on 2010, and the dependent of insured filed an application for the insurance claim. On investigation, it was found that the claimant had an already existing policy with Max New York life insurance. The claim to insurance as well as the premium paid were repudiated, and the court ruled in favor of Reliance life insurance.

Case of Sushila Indravadan Gandhi and Anr. vs New India Assurance Co. Ltd. and others (2020).

In this case, a doctor died due to negligence of the ambulance driver. The issue was not with the claim, but rather with the interpretation of whether the doctor worked with the hospital or worked for the hospital. This distinction can make a difference on whether the insurance company is liable to paying the claim or not.

The judgment was in favor of the nominee of the deceased, and the claim was honored. As we can see with the above cases, the underwriting process is very complicated and needs meticulous focus and attention. An AI system that can act as supplementary information provider to the underwriter and help with risk analysis can be very useful in this scenario.

7.3.4 Classification of Risks

An actuary has to analyze multiple number of risks. Both insurance risks and market risks are considered by an actuary in his/her own capacities. However, while dealing with a specific problem, the point of failure has to be prioritized. Every risk can be categorized based on the likelihood of its occurrence as well as the impact of its occurrence. Identification of internal and external risks and being able to categorize risks on the basis of the likelihood of the risk impacting the investment is one of the functions of an actuary.

Classification of risks is done by actuaries on the basis of what they have been trained in and with the guidance of their superior officers, as well as some industry standards. Introduction of AI to this context would make the process of classification not only simpler but also more accurate. Risk classification on the basis of hard evidence by the AI models can also prove useful in the following circumstances.

In the future, actuaries may not be called on often to “predict” because the machines will have already done so. What will become more valuable is what is done with that prediction (Curtis 2020).

7.3.5 Fraud Detection

Fraud is one among the many possible sources of damage to the firm or a company, which lends or insures a property or life. But fraud is also one of the complicated issues for a company to resolve. The early detection of fraud can come off as doubting the customer, and this can cost heavily if the doubts are unfounded. These issues can cost the company as well as the trust of the consumer if not handled properly. On the other hand, being too lenient with the customer with a detection system might lead to an increase in the number of frauds happening. Deloitte’s insurance fraud survey of 2023 revealed that insurance companies in India have experienced a rise in instances of fraud within the life and health insurance categories.

An AI system that could model behaviors and risks could be very helpful in this scenario to warn the company of an impending fraud or the likelihood of the consumer to be a part of a fraud.

By developing a data-based classification function that can be applied to existing and new claims, fraud examiners can continuously and automatically monitor fraud levels (which is useful because of the huge number of claims files daily) and signal suspicious claims as they come in (Ai et al. 2009).

There are a few cases in the Indian context that have been brought to the court where the presence of AI could make a difference. Some of these frauds include:

  • Application fraud
  • Claims fraud
  • Forgery
  • Phony policy fraud
  • Identity theft

7.3.6 Case Studies for Fraud and Categorizing Risks

After interviewing an insurance officer from a reputed insurance company in India, we were able to gather some insights about the frauds that used to happen in the insurance system. One claimant had made 3 different policies for his father from different branches of the same company and claimed the insurance money. The insurance company had to visit and gain some information from the claimant about some other issue, where it was found out that the father was still alive and healthy.

While talking to an agent, it was the father himself who asked the agent to see how his other 3 insurance schemes were doing. Later, the agent while going through the system recognized that the claim has been collected and the presumed dead person was the one who spoke with the agent.

The company was able to catch hold of the fraudster. Due to improvements in technology and centralization of data, this fraud is not possible for anyone to do anymore, but it does provide a window into how loopholes can be found in a system with a huge repository of data.

7.3.7 Categorize Risk and Pricing Details of Premiums

To categorize risk and create a pricing of the premiums is also another function of the actuary. The majority of the time, this is done by using the company guidelines as a reference to categorize risk and have a pricing detail for the premium.

The system used for pricing is very similar to bundle pricing where an entire bundle is sold off at a pre-allotted price. The pricing model does not allow for variability in customization. AI can be used in this particular context to create tailor-made customization of pricing of premiums as well as to categorize risks with more accuracy and efficiency.

There have been various cases in India where the customer insures an asset and then intentionally damages it to claim the insurance. These kinds of instances are referred to as fabricated accidents. This usually occurs in circumstances involving business where the sum promised does not hold good anymore or the created product lacks in quality. Suppression of material facts regarding the type of damage to the asset that has occurred usually gets revealed later during the litigation or enquiry process.

There are also instances where hospitals are involved in reframing the cost of the procedure in order to claim more insurance amount from the companies. An AI system that could act as an early detection system to catch these discrepancies and warn the higher officials, as well as act as a support system for decision-making for field agents that could highlight the likelihood of occurrence of problems, would be very impactful in this area.

7.3.8 Asset and Investment Models

Insurance companies will collect premium from their customers from various types of plans. The plans might be for life, accident, vehicle, marine, general, health, etc. This will result in an accumulation of funds in the hands of the insurance companies. Now the responsibility of utilizing these funds, investing them, and diversifying the portfolio to maximize the returns in order to both make a profit margin as well as provide coverage to the claims lies in the hands of actuaries.

Without an actuary and without the knowledge possessed by an actuary, the insurance companies would not be able to efficiently handle the risks involved and fail to provide financial stability to both their customers as well as their assets. Actuaries use statistical models to summarize micro-level data, which subsequently need to be interpreted properly for financial decision-making (Frees et al. 2009).

Actuaries not only have to be well versed in the field of insurance and risk analysis for creation of pricing of premiums and claims, but they also have to be very knowledgeable in the field of finance. Most importantly with regard to investment avenues, their role is critical. Financial modeling, financial forecasting, capital budgeting decision-making, corporate restructuring, raising of funds through different sources or avenues, dividend policies and dividend decisions, and hedging techniques are all major functions of an actuary in the field of finance. They are also required to analyze the financial stability and performance of a business organization. These functions are completed by using statistical tools and complex models.

With the above list of functions associated with actuaries, it becomes quite apparent how important the role of an actuary is for an insurance company. A provision of an AI cannot replace an actuary, but it can help provide various parameters which help in decision-making for the actuary and provide relevant details and calculations so that the actuary can focus on the crucial matters that affect the company.

7.3.9 Insolvency and Projected Liabilities

Insolvency is the state of incapacity of the individual or a company to repay the debt taken. Insolvency can affect an insurance-based entity or a financial entity in a variety of ways.

The firm that the insurance company has invested in can become bankrupt which in turn makes it incapable of producing returns for the insurance company. Or the other scenario where an insurance company has to deal with insolvency is when a client, whether individual or a company, is unable or unwilling to pay the premiums which were decided as per the terms of the contract of insurance. This is when the projected liability becomes risky, increasing financial burden on the insurance company.

The period for payment of premiums is set by insurance companies. The insurance companies are also expected to provide a grace period in order to provide for payments in case of defaults in payments. If the premiums have not been paid at all in the span of 3 consecutive years since the beginning of the policy, then the insurance contract becomes void with immediate effect.

It is left to the discretion of insurance companies whether they want to provide a clause within the contract for reviving policies within specified periods with the payment of pending premiums.

If the customer is not able to pay the premiums even after 3 years and the period allotted for revival of the insurance, then the insurance contract is terminated and the policyholder is provided the surrender value.

The insurance companies can still provide opportunities for re-insurance by including penalties, new medical check-ups, applying charges and fines, etc.

The reason why insurance companies provide so many opportunities for non-payment of premiums is because it is much easier to maintain an older customer and onboard the older customer onto new plans and choices, while the same is difficult for new customers (Grosen and Jørgensen 2016) .

An AI system that could provide the associated risks based on history and personality of the customer or even a statistical model of a company to understand the acceptance the risks associated with investing into it can be very impactful in the field of actuarial science. As actuarial analysis and evaluation is a time-consuming, complex, and error-prone process, it can be improved or enhanced considerably by automated reasoning (Zhou & Ashby 2009).

7.3.10 Pension

The amount of money that is paid to an employee in the form of benefits at the time of retirement or after the time of retirement is called pension. There are two types of pensions:

  • Defined contribution plan
  • Defined benefit plan

7.3.10.1 Defined Contribution Plan

It is a plan where both the employer and the employee contribute a fixed amount of payment each month that is pooled and invested into the market by the use of various instruments based on risk tolerance of the employee. During the time of the retirement, the lumpsum amount is paid back to the employee.

Provident fund contribution is the best example for Defined contribution plan in India.

In this type of plan, the employer does not assume much amount of risk.

7.3.10.2 Defined Benefit Plan

A Defined Benefit Plan is however different from defined contribution plan in the sense that the employer promises or guarantees the employee a fixed amount of lumpsum at the end of the duration of the career regardless of the market fluctuations and company conditions.

In this type of pension, the employer shoulders the majority portion of the risk. Gratuity is the best example for Defined Benefit Plan in India.

In designing the defined benefit plan, the role of an actuary is very important. By considering different factors of the employee like age, gender salary, expected retirement date or time, and expected return on pension plan investments, the actuary is able to predict how much amount is to be contributed by the employer to a specific fund to fulfil the obligations guaranteed to the employee in the form of pensions.

The main considerations that are looked at by the actuary in order to create a model for prediction of the status of the fund are:

  • Funding ratio
  • Mortality tables
  • Investment strategy
  • Actuarial assumptions

Funding ratio refers to the ratio which determines the amount of assets to the amount of liabilities of a defined plan. This ratio is used by the actuary to understand whether contributions made to the fund are accurate. The fund can either found to be underpaid or overpaid. If the fund is underpaid, then the employer has to push more contribution to the fund. However, if the fund is overpaid, then the employer can take a contribution holiday and not pay the fund for a limited period of time as it is performing well on its own (England and Verrall 2006).

Different countries have different frequencies at which an actuary gets consulted for analyzing the direct benefit plan. In general, the standard time frequency for an actuarial consultation is 3 years to assess the funding ratio.

Mortality tables are tables used to predict life expectancy. By using statistical tools and historical data in the context of pensions, the life expectancy of a pensioner after retirement is taken into consideration. Mortality tables can help the actuary to predict the duration for which the company has to pay the pensioner after retirement and calculate the obligation of the company to the collection of fund during the present.

Investment strategies refer to the strategies used by the employer and their ability to handle risks in long-term duration. The actuary has to not only understand the risk tolerance or risk averseness in the strategies used by the employer but also predict the outcomes of these risks in the volatile market. After calculating the risks involved, the actuary then has to recommend whether to increase or decrease contributions to the fund in order to fulfil the expected obligation.

The amount of additional funding needed to supplement the specified future contributions is called the “unfunded liability”. The unfunded liability can change in a funding method, not because of any reassessment of what has happened in the past, but because different methods promise different contributions in the future (Ezra, 1980).

An actuary makes a few assumptions to estimate the performance of the pension plan in the future, and these assumptions are called actuarial assumptions. These assumptions are based on certain factors like expected rate of return of the fund, economic conditions like inflation rates and interest rates, increment in salary component of an employee, trends in government regulations, etc.

The actuary has to be aware of the risks involved in the market as well as the variations in life expectancies. The statistical models for these purposes can get extremely complicated and here is where AI can lift the weight off the shoulders of an actuary.

7.3.10.2.1 Awareness

According to the “Pre launch survey report of insurance awareness campaign” sponsored by IRDAI and NCAER and conducted under the leadership of Anushree Sinha, even though a majority of Indian households are good savers, they do not undertake financial planning and are financially at risk. Households need to understand the risk of both “living too long” and “dying too young” (Sinha 2016).

The survey had a sample size of 15,000 plus families in India, and it accurately depicts the amount of awareness about insurance among the insured and uninsured families in India. Awareness about Insurance, the importance of insurance, and the paperwork involved as well as the general workings of scheme is extremely beneficial to the general audience. It increases financial literacy, makes way for good decision-making, and well-educated and informed audiences can make economic ripples in the society.

Due to the sheer number of policies, insurance companies, and products that are available in the catalog for the audience to choose from, good choices are possible only if there is proper awareness. Even with proper awareness about policies, it’s also important that the audience has awareness about what policies suit their needs the best.

The customers should also be aware of government institutions that listen to grievances about policies or insurance companies. This reduces the uncertainty that comes along while investing money into insurance schemes and provides a guarantee. The audience should also be made aware of clauses in the policy about revival of the insurance, surrender value, and other factors.

Overall, the amount of knowledge around insurance schemes and products is immense, and to make an informed decision, the awareness required by an average customer is way higher than the present levels. As of now, the majority of the effort is shouldered by the agents and government institutions like IRDA, but even this platform has its own disadvantages.

Awareness derived from agents depends heavily upon the knowledge and skill of the insurance agent himself/herself. And awareness from institutions is not personalized enough for the common man to gain insight that is required individually. AI-based chatbots that are fed with information can be of immense help in this area as they can provide personalized and unbiased information and can also provide so in multiple regional languages which increases the reach of the knowledge to both rural and urban areas.

7.4 Conclusion

In conclusion, the application of AI in the field of actuarial science is inevitable. The amount of convenience it provides in terms of efficiency, financial flexibility, and decision-making is nowhere comparable to raw human input. However, AI here can be used only as a supplementary mode of value addition, and hoping to replace actuaries altogether by AI systems might be far-fetched at the moment. It is true that the AI systems are vastly more capable and faster than the present systems, be it underwriters, agents, actuaries, etc., but the ground realities are most well understood by real people. The actual opportunity here is the combination of AI and its technical prowess, along with the foresight and inherent utility of people.

8 Role of AI in Insurance IndustryScope, Benefits, and Adoption Roadmap

Kumar Shalender and Rajesh Kumar Yadav

DOI: 10.1201/9781003472551-10

8.1 Introduction

The sweeping transformation that artificial intelligence (AI) is heralding across the industrial sectors is apparent today. Like other industries, the insurance sector is also experiencing transformative change thanks to using AI in its everyday operations. Specifically, the significance of AI in claim processing and management is nothing less than that of a miracle. AI has multiple capabilities of automated collection and analyses of data which can prove to be extremely beneficial for the insurance companies and associated functionaries and partners in the industry (Fathana and Fathana 2023). It is not an exaggeration to say that the role of AI is becoming central to the insurance industry. AI professionals can quickly take into consideration a large number of parameters of critical performance and map these dimensions against the current and emerging future scenarios to help companies make profitable business decisions. The stream of decision-making in insurance in the past was not very complex due to a limited number of variables and dimensions, but with time, the decisions related to the insurance claims, their processing, and analysis are becoming increasingly complex. AI can solve many complexities and challenges in the insurance industry. The technology uses algorithms to collect, analyse, and evaluate the data, which is very helpful for the insurance companies. In conjunction with ML, AI technology can also predict future scenarios and help stakeholders achieve better planning and operational capability (Shalender et al. 2023). By offering insights into the application potential of AI in the insurance industry, the study offers crucial implications for all the stakeholders in the business ecosystem. From research scholars and practitioners to regulatory agencies and policymakers, the chapter will help every stakeholder to integrate the application of AI in insurance and how this technology can bring numerous benefits to the doorsteps of the industry by enhancing the productivity and profitability of insurance companies’ operations in the sector (Grover and Shalender 2023). Looking into the different prospective applications of AI, this study is expected to help the insurance industry develop an understanding and put in place the functional and operational mechanisms required for integrating AI into various aspects of doing their business (Gareiss 2023). Arguably, the most important benefit of this integration will manifest in terms of increasing the accuracy and predictive analysis of insurance companies as these parameters are of utmost importance when it comes to ensuring the sustainability of the business going forward.

8.2 AI in the Insurance Industry: Game Changing Benefits

8.2.1 Enhancing Productivity

By streamlining the entire cycle of raising the claim, assessing the claim, and settling claims by making the payment, AI can significantly enhance the productivity of insurance operations. As the number of insurance claims filed by claimants continues to reach a new high by the day, the application of AI can complement productivity by automating the process of assessing and evaluating a large number of standard claims that would have otherwise required human interventions (Kulshrestha et al. 2022). While it is important to understand that for resolving some specific discreet cases, human intervention is very much required. However, for other standardised scenarios, the majority of the claims can be processed with the help of AI, which not only helps organisations achieve a higher level of productivity and customer satisfaction, but also enhances customer satisfaction by crediting the money into the account for beneficiaries in a quick manner (Goyal 2023). AI can also be applied to fully digitise the entire mechanism of filing and processing claims while aiding underwriters to make superior business decisions by supplementing them with accurate predictive capabilities associated with their clientele. This increase in productivity can be turned into superior business results for insurance companies which will help them stay competitive in the long run (Aggarwal et al. 2023).

8.2.2 Predictive Analysis

AI can help insurance firms not only collect relevant data about current scenarios but also extrapolate to get important insights related to the future. Insurance companies need to make a comprehensive analysis of the business environment while taking into consideration the prospects of natural calamities, uncertain weather, and other uncontrollable variables associated with the business environment. The accuracy of the predictive analysis is very important for insurance firms as it helps them manage their operations effectively in the future (Shalender 2023). Take, for instance, the state of Florida in the US, where insurance companies are increasingly finding it difficult to offer policies because of the uncertain weather conditions. This is true for many other parts of the world including countries like Australia, New Zealand, and Canada. AI can help insurance companies address this challenge by offering accurate and precise features in Adios related to weather patterns, geological developments, and associated phenomena (Shalender and Sharma 2021). In conjunction with various IoT devices that can record weather data, AI can help insurance companies accurately predict future patterns and offer precise insurance quotes for prospective customers. The large and unprecedented quantity of data collected by drones, IoT, remote sensing, and other allied technologies can be easily analysed with the help of AI, and accordingly, insurance premiums can be decided realistically and accurately.

8.2.3 Exploring New Segments

AI can prove immensely beneficial for insurance firms to capitalise on new areas of business development that are allied with changing trends in customer and business space. Take, for instance, the growing prominence of cybersecurity. The wave of digitalisation is sweeping through the world, and with more people embracing the digital revolution with open arms, the threats related to cybersecurity are intensifying at an alarming speed (Shalender et al. 2023). AI can help insurance companies analyse the potential of cybersecurity space as a promising business category and help them develop this as an attractive business opportunity (Mittal et al. 2019). By offering actionable insights, AI can help insurance analyse their competencies and match them with various components of the external environment (Pangilinan 2023). The AI application in insurance will also equip category to analyse their current portfolio and then decide which categories they should expand the business or divest the services to run an overall profitable business. Similarly, emerging areas such as blockchain technology, non-fungible tokens (NFTs), and metaverse can be potentially analysed with the help of AI technology to understand their profit potential if insurance firms decide to make a foray into these categories.

8.2.4 Offering Flexible Options

AI can help insurance companies offer flexible insurance with a variety of premium payment options. Many insurance firms in the health category are now using smart apps, phones, and wearable devices to track the personal health of insured persons (Shalender and Yadav 2019). These habits, eating patterns, and associated activities of the insured persons are then analysed with the help of AI, and in line with the results, flexible options related to the insurance policies are being issued to customers (Shalender et al. 2023). The persons exhibiting safer and health-conscious behaviour might be offered lower premium payments, whereas others with high risky behaviour might be charged more as the likelihood of these people making the claim is greater than those of others. Rewarding those who are exhibiting safer and healthier options in their life is likely to inspire others to exhibit the same kind of behaviour and will help in the propagation of good practices among society members. This incentivisation can be adopted at a larger scale, and a push in this regard by the policymakers and regulators can help in motivating the society to stick to the good practices and also get rewarded in the process (Shalender and Shankar 2023). Specifically, in the development of the health insurance, the existing data related to customer attitudes and behaviour patterns can prove instrumental in enhancing the overall innovative capabilities of the segment.

8.2.5 Enhancing Customer Service

AI-enabled chatbots are increasingly replacing human intervention in customer service, and by speedily solving the complex issues associated with claims and processing, these can prove instrumental in enhancing customer satisfaction related to the insurance industry. No wonder, today we find an increasing number of insurance companies employing AI-powered chatbots for offering a complete constellation of services to the target market (Sharma et al. 2023). Unlike human resistance, AI can work around the clock, which is fantastic for companies that offer global insurance services in different parts of the world. The small and mundane issues related to the insurance sector such as difficulty in logging in, raising claims, processing requests, and asking for customer assistance can be easily solved with the help of automated chatbots. Having said that, insurance companies must realise that the use of chatbots should be assisted with human resources of the organisation. Especially when there is a query that is not standardised, the need to supplement the information manually by looking into different aspects of the problem has to be considered (Shalender et al. 2023). Therefore, insurance companies need to create a mix of automated chatbots and human resources that come together to offer better service experiences to the target market.

8.3 AI in the Insurance Industry: Recommended Strategies and Adoption Roadmap

The following strategies (shown in Figure 8.1) will help insurance firms to adopt AI for their business ecosystem. By focusing on the specific steps, organisations will be able to leverage the immense potential associated with this new-age technology and achieve considerably superior results on multiple fronts such as business development, client acquisitions, and enhancing claims accuracy.

8.3.1 Start Internally

First and foremost, insurance companies should start by sensitising their employees about the immense potential of AI and the multiple benefits associated with its use cases. As insurers start integrating the practices of AI in their functional procedures and operational mechanisms, it will be important to involve the entire workforce so that the desirable results on efficiency and effectiveness can be achieved. The application of AI requires a considerable change in existing business processes, which means employees of the organisation have to bear with the change in their routine work. In the absence of motivation and encouragement, the employees might not be able to cope with the changes, and consequently, insurance firms won’t be able to derive the desired benefits out of the adoption and integration of AI in their business processes. Also, it is important to make sure that employees are well-prepared to use AI in their operational procedures (Phansis 2023). To that end, organisations might be required to arrange various training sessions by industrial experts to enhance the current level of knowledge and information of their employees related to AI practices. It is very important to emphasise the technical skills related to the use of AI as without mastering the technicalities associated with the use of new technology, it might not be possible for employees to derive maximum benefit from the technology (Sharma et al. 2023).

8.3.2 Create Compliance

The regulatory norms related to the use of AI Technology are not standardised across the globe. In the insurance industry, no clear-cut rules and regulations have been framed when it comes to use of the AI by the industry firms. In such an environment, insurance companies might be required to come out with a clear AI compliance plan that will help organisations in the adoption of the technology without breaking the rules and regulations related to it (Shnaps 2023). This will not only clear the air when it comes to the adoption of AI within the organisation but also inspire other companies in the sector to follow the same strategy for encouraging the widespread adoption of AI in the industrial ecosystem (Snape 2023). While there are many dimensions associated with this adoption of AI, specific requirements to use AI for risk assessment and claim management processes need to be focused on, as these two critical areas can make or break the adoption prospects of AI in the industry. The development of such compliance by the players of the insurance industry will also encourage policymakers to deliberate on the matter and offer a framework that will help the integration of AI and other new-age technologies for the achievement of business results for participant stakeholders in the business ecosystem of the insurance industry.

8.3.3 Prioritise Areas

As shown in Figure 8.1, one of the practical and more realistic ways of adopting AI in the insurance industry is to start small and use the technology to experiment with less risky functions. For example, rather than outrightly using AI for processing of claims and clearance of payment, it is better to start with using the technology for collection of the data and analysis of the claims. Replacing human resources with AI technology in these less risky affairs will help the company to closely follow the integration of AI in the business and its impact on the profitability and operating efficiency of the company.

The use of AI in less risky areas also helps companies to become confident in the application of the technology in high-risk functions which are directly associated with claim processing and payment processing, among others. Remember, successful AI implementation should start small and use the insights derived from its applications in less-risk areas to make a robust system that will ultimately pave the way for its use in high-risk functions as well. Using AI in less-risk areas will make the employees of the company confident about the application prospects of the technology and will encourage them to learn the technicalities behind the application of AI in the sector (Sharma et al. 2020). Such moves, therefore, will prove conducive to the overall deployment of the technology in the insurance industry and will help the sector embrace the technology going forward.

8.3.4 Data Usage

The quality of data has important impact on the effectiveness of AI. The use of genuine data will offer accurate results while the fake data will yield wrong information. It is, therefore, essential for the insurance industry to use accurate data for right decision-making. The collection of the data needs to be done in a careful manner, and there must be quality checks for ensuring that only authentic data is used in decision-making. Safeguard measures should be taken and quality checks conducted for getting genuine data (Vyas et al. 2023). Data continuously remains at the centre of attention for policymakers and regulators, and any kind of discrepancy about the data might prove very harmful for the organisations. Hence, it becomes mandatory for organisations in the insurance sector to use AI to be very careful about the data structures and patterns and must work in collaboration with all the stakeholders in the ecosystem so that any leakage of sensitive data or information can be prevented right at its source.

8.4 Discussion

AI in the insurance sector can bring many benefits to the table. Arguably, the most significant benefit associated with technology usage is the optimum utilisation of the available resources, which not only helps elevate efficiency but also reduces the wastages associated with business processes. Insurance firms should consider the adoption of AI in the insurance industry from a strategic viewpoint because this adoption is a long journey that will require commitment from all participating stakeholders for a long period. It is a process that involves the entire organisation, needs top management support, and takes time before offering visible improvements in various processes and procedures. Therefore, it is required for all the stakeholders in the insurance industry, especially the shareholders to commit to the integration process from a long-time perspective. The enormous growth prospects that organisations can easily reap by integrating AI into their business models, which is one of the sure-shot ways to convince shareholders about the adoption of AI. It is also important to understand that the implementation process will not be smooth, and organisations might also encounter some resistance from other stakeholders in the value chain while going ahead with the integration process. Production companies, supply chain intermediaries, and even consumers might oppose the idea of integrating AI in case they encounter any kind of inconvenience or time delay associated with the process. Hence, companies need to understand that before venturing out with the integration of AI in the customer segments, they must do their proper homework so that the integration and implementation can be achieved smoothly and seamlessly. This particular preparation will make sure that the adoption process related to AI will reach the customers as a novel measure that helps businesses achieve superior results. Given the fact that the regulatory environment is not very clear, the critical responsibility lies on the shoulders of policymakers to come up with clear regulations in this regard. By offering clarity about the rules related to AI and its use, governments worldwide can help the sector achieve higher growth and profitability while reaping more dividends in terms of the tax income generated by the sector. The support of the policymakers is also important in terms of making common citizens aware of the huge potential that AI offers to prospective insurance seekers.

8.5 Conclusion

The significance of AI as a technology in the field of insurance is lighting to gain momentum going forward. The adoption drive will be primarily felicitated by the fact that consumers are increasingly demanding more convenience from the insurance sector, especially when it comes to completing the processes associated with raising the claims, processing the applications, and releasing the claimed money to the account of the customers. The AI can fully automate the entire cycle, and by delivering enhanced comfort and convenience to companies and customers alike, it can strengthen the brand equity of insurance organisations while making the consumers more satisfied with the services of insurance companies. To summarise, AI in the insurance sector can do wonders, and by creating a mutually beneficial relationship throughout the value chain, this integration can help the insurance industry to offer more value-oriented and effective services to customers.

9 AI’s Role in Improving the Quality of Insurance Actuarial Services

Gurpreet Kaur, Puneet Singh, and Anshull Bhalla

DOI: 10.1201/9781003472551-11

9.1 Introduction

In the present scenario wherein there is rapid technological development, the insurance industry is heading towards innovation by using AI for improvising the insurance services (Naim, 2022). With the development of AI technologies, there is vast transformation in the existing traditional insurance practices by bringing in opportunities that would ensure smooth processes for mitigating risks, streamlining operations, and better customer experiences (Holland and Kavuri, 2023).

The present study brings insights into the vital role of AI in bringing about revolutionizing change in the insurance industry, improving actuarial services covering the wide areas.

The application of AI enhances customer satisfaction and increases the efficiency and accuracy of insurance activities such as underwriting (Eling et al., 2022), fraud detection (Agarwal, 2023), and claims processing (Dhashanamoorthi, 2023).

Besides this, the subsets of AI such as machine learning, predictive analytics, and natural language processing provide additional benefits by giving better insights that enable better decision-making.

Furthermore, the integration of AI-powered solutions and chatbots has brought about revolution in customer interactions (Fong, 2023). This will further provide strategies to mitigate risk proactively (Gupta, 2020). The present paper aims to provide in-depth insight into the impact of AI in the insurance industry. By embracing AI-driven innovations, insurers can not only enhance operational efficiency and mitigate risks but also foster greater trust and loyalty (Bodemer, 2023) among customers in an increasingly competitive marketplace. The chapter is segregated on the following grounds. Section 9.1 provides the introduction to the study. Section 9.2 brings about justification from past studies. Section 9.3 presents an interpretation of past studies. Sections 9.4 and 9.5 deal with conclusion, recommendations, and future scope of the study. Section 9.6 provides references for the study.

9.2 Review of Literature

Many studies in the past have been conducted, which provides in-depth analysis of how AI application has improvised the industry sector (Singh and Chivukula, 2020). The literature provides path for improvising the insurance industry by adopting new technologies such as blockchain and smart contracts. The studies from past enumerating the role of AI in providing better customer services are mentioned below.

Agrawal et al. (2024) explore how better AI predictions impact decision-making and risk management (focusing on risk, task connections, and existing uncertainty processes) using a model to analyse AI adoption. Khalisa (2024) explores how digitalisation and AI impact the insurance broker industry, using interviews to investigate if AI can replace brokers, but concludes that AI enhances efficiency while human expertise remains crucial. Ahmad et al. (2024) analyse secondary data to assess how InsurTech advancements improve efficiency in the insurance industry, examining the benefits for both insurers and consumers in a multi-perspective approach.

Srinivas and Gandra (2023) examine AI use in Andhra Pradesh insurance, assessing the use of AI for sustainability using surveys and data to recommend actions for policymakers. Amerirad et al. (2023) investigate how malicious actors can manipulate AI algorithms in the insurance industry, exploring specific vulnerabilities and potential solutions for fraud prevention. Fong (2023) explores AI for insurance agents, aiming to boost sales and efficiency for a stronger economy through increased insurance use in Malaysia. Bodemer (2023) combines blockchain and AI-enabled smart contracts to tackle insurance issues, finding that it improves transparency, reduces fraud, and optimizes risk assessment (mixed methods). Dhashanamoorthi (2023) investigates leveraging AI to streamline dispute resolution in road traffic accident insurance claims, proposing a method for cost estimation and negotiation to expedite the process. Komperla (2022) explores AI’s applications and limitations, finding that it will likely assist doctors, not replace them, due to the importance of human interaction.

Khan and Khan (2023) explore the potential of AI in healthcare, examining its applications and limitations, and conclude that AI will likely augment doctors’ capabilities rather than replace them entirely due to the importance of the human touch. Zhang et al. (2023) explores AI’s potential to expedite RTA insurance claim resolution through real-case analysis and AI tools, aiming to benefit all parties.

Benedek and Nagy (2023) examine if AI is more cost-effective than traditional methods for car insurance fraud detection, using real data to show that traditional methods are currently better. Trivino (2023) examines AI’s applications in insurance, including its impact on pricing, service, and technology use (analytics, geospatial), and he also highlights ethical considerations like fairness, privacy, and bias in AI use. Agarwal (2023) explores advanced machine learning to create a fraud detection system for medical claims, aiming to boost accuracy and efficiency in insurance and potentially improve healthcare systems.

Pradeep and Patil (2022) analyse the use of AI in insurance (including healthcare) across global firms, concluding that AI adoption is essential for financial institutions to compete globally despite challenges. Owens et al. (2022) investigate how explainable AI is used in insurance, focusing on methods like knowledge distillation, and find them common in claims, underwriting, and pricing. Aslam et al. (2022) build a system to spot auto insurance fraud using predictive models (like logistic regression) and show its promise for real-time detection.

Rukhsar et al. (2022; Dora et al., 2022) compare machine learning techniques to detect insurance fraud, aiming to benefit both insurance and healthcare industries. Lupačov and Stanković (2022) analyse AI’s growing role in insurance, exploring its applications, tools, and implementation, concluding that it holds immense potential for the industry. Holland (2022) examines BGL Group (European insurance leader) use of AI to create a general AI customer lifecycle model applicable across the insurance industry, while also exploring future directions for AI in this field. Alli et al. (2021) examine Nigerian insurance’s view of AI and its impact on claims, marketing, service, and underwriting. It finds that AI can significantly improve these areas using surveys and analysis. Eling et al. (2021) examine how AI impacts insurance using existing frameworks, analysing data from various sources, and concludes that AI offers cost savings, new revenue, and a shift from reacting to losses to predicting and preventing them.

Prajapati (2022) explores how AI, machine learning, and big data are used by insurers to enhance customer satisfaction, analytics, and trend identification. Lin et al. (2020) explore how applying AI algorithms’ reasoning capabilities to analyse the link between AI and finance can significantly improve income generation within the financial field. Zarifis et al. (2023) investigate how the implementation of AI automation in insurance companies is leading to the emergence of four distinct business models within the sector.

AI-enabled technology has proved to be very effective in improvising the customer sector. All the above studies focused on the application of AI in insurance. AI has brought about more accuracy and transparency in customer services.

9.3 Research Methodology

The paper is based on secondary data which is collected from Journals, books, websites, etc. The paper describes the applications of AI in enhancing the actuarial services of the insurance industry.

9.5 Conclusion and Recommendation

The technological development has given rise to numerous opportunities for the insurance to holistically develop and grow in its sphere. The studies have concluded that AI application has enabled coordinated decision-making (Agrawal et al., 2024) by forecasting the future. AI, by integrating with blockchain smart contracts, has a significant potential to build up more transparency, integrity, and safety to data stored. Further, it has led to speedy risk assessment, claim processing, underwriting, etc., which further enhances productivity and provides better customer services ensuring business growth and success (Sundari, 2024). Besides this, it is also observed that AI has led to better retention of clients and also attracted new client base which increased the premium level intensively (Ahmad et al., 2024). However, as the insurance landscape evolves with AI, it also presents new challenges and ethical considerations. From concerns surrounding data privacy and algorithmic bias to regulatory compliance (Bodemer, 2023) and cybersecurity risks, insurers must navigate a complex ecosystem to harness the full potential of AI while upholding trust and transparency (Bodemer, 2023). AI strategies have improvised decision-making, boosting the insurance efficiency and customer satisfaction (Komperla, 2022). The studies conclude that the insurance sector has great potential and opportunities for improving the market penetration by using AI (Fong, 2023) and so it is recommended that an awareness programme should be developed to educate clients on the adoption of AI technologies (Owens et al., 2022; Radu and Alexandru, 2022).

9.6 Scope for Future Research

Despite so many potential benefits of AI, the adoption of AI is still in its infancy due to several challenges such as a lack of awareness and a lack of well-defined AI-enabled technology usage. Therefore, the study suggests that the future researchers should focus on estimating the influence of AI adoption on insurance protection on broader parameters. Owens et al. (2022) and Stern et al. (2022) explained factors that will help in overcoming the challenges in adoption of AI by the insurance industry.
